GENERAL STATEMENT IN RELATION TO
UNIDENTIFIED FLYING OBJECTS
ст. 15002
580/1/1/41
1.
Your report on the sighting of an Unusual Flying Objeet (UFO)
has been investigated and the object was has not been, deterpined at. this time.
•••
•. •
.. •
2.
You may be interested in the investigation of reports in Australia,
the United States and the United Kingdom and the following information provides
a brief outline of the subject.
3.
The investigation of reports of UFOs in Australia is carried out by
the Royal Austtalian Air Force, Directorate of Air Force Intelligence, at the
Department of Air in Canberra. A considerable amount of effort is spent in
investigating each report and the majority of observers are interviewed by
selected RAAT
personnel.
4.
Between 23 January 60 and 26 May 71, the RAAF received 572 UFO
reports. Department of Air has assessed that 93 percent were explainable by
present scientific knowledge. Six percent of the reports did not provide
sufficient information to permit proper analysis and evaluation. One percent
of the reports were attributed to unknown causes.
5.
The United Kingdom Air Ministry has stated that on an average, 90
percent of the sightings it investigates are explained by causes ranging from
meteorological balloons and meteors to aircraft lights. The other 10 percent
probably remain unexplained because of lack of reliable information.
6.
In 1966 the University of Colorado, under contract to the USAF,
spent two years processing and investigating all American reports. Its
findings have been published by Bantam Books as 'The Condon Report!. Its
general conclusion is that 'nothing has come from the study of UFOs in the
past 21 years that has added to seientific knowledge and that further
extensive study of UFOs probably cannot be
justified in the expectation that
science will be advanced thereby'. A panel of eminent seientists, chosen by
the US National Academy of Sciences, has
examined and endorsed the Condon Report.
/. The

ст 18800
-2-
7.
The USAF (Project Blue Book investigation of unidentified aerial
objects
between 1953 and 1965 analysed 7,641 UFO reports.
a.
80 percent of
sightings were natural phenomena,
hoaxes, birds or man-made objects;
b.
17 percent of sightings provided insufficient data
It concluded
that:
to permit thorough analysis and evaluation; and
three percent were unidentified.
8.
US and Soviet space exploration has found no evidence to support
the theory of life on planets in our solar
system. It seems that the Mariner
series of
space exploration to Mars has proved it a dead planet. The only
other source of extra-terrestrial life, then, would have to be in another
'solar system'. It would seem, therefore, that whilst it may be possible for
extra-terrestrial life forms to visit Earth, it is improbable.
Department of Air
CANBERRA

REPORT OF UNUSUAL AERIAL OBJECE

GENERAL STATEMENT IN RELATION TO
UNIDENTIFIED
FLYING OBJECTS
1.
Your report on the
sighting of an Unusual Flying Object (UPO)
has been investigated and the object was..! they, BaTe..
OVER
... 9: 900007
PATCHES
2.
You may be interested in the investigation of reports in Australia,
the United States and the United Kingdom and the following information provides
a brief outline of the subject.
3.
The investigation of reports of UFOs in Australia is carried out by
the Royal Australian Air Force, Directorate of Air Force Intelligence, at the
Department of Air in Canberra. A considerable amount of effort is spent in
investigating each report and the majority of observers are interviewed by
selected RAAF personnel.
4.
Between 23 January 60 and 26 May 71, the RAAF received 572 UFO
reports. Department of Air has assessed that 93 percent were explainable by
present scientific knowledge. Six percent of the reports did not provide
sufficient information to pernit proper analysis and evaluation. One percent
of the reports were attributed to unknown causes.
5.
The United Kingdom Air Ministry has stated that on an average, 90
percent of the sightings it investigates are explained by causes ranging from
meteorological balloons and meteors to aircraft lights. The other 10 percent
probably remain unexplained because of lack of reliable information.
In 1966 the University of Colorado, under contract to the USAF,
spent two years processing and investigating all American reports.
Its
findings have been published by Bantam Books as The Condon Report' Ita
general conclusion is that nothing has come from the study of UFOs in the
past 21 years that has added to
seientific knowledge! and that 'further
extensive study of UFOs probably cannot be justified in the expectation that
science will be advanced thereby!. A panel of eminent scientists, chosen by
the US National Academy of Sciences, has examined and endorsed the Condon Report+
/7. The

investigation of unidentified aerial
objects between 1953 and 1965 analysed 7,641 UFO reports. It concluded that:

b.
17 pereent of sightings provided insufficient data
to permit thorough analysis and evaluation;
and
c.
three percent were unidentified.
8.
US and Soviet space exploration has found no evidence to support
the theory of life on planets in our solar system. It seems that the Mariner
series of space exploration to Mars has proved it
a dead" planet.
The only
other source of extra-terrestrial life, then, would have to be in another
'solar system'. It would seem, therefore, that whilst it may be possible for
extra-terrestrial life forms to visit Earth, it is improbable.

The question of "co-operation" between the RAAF and local
UFO groups would then be a matter for speculation and individual interpret-
This Headquarters therefore deemed it prudent not to
initiate an on site investigation into the incident at the time.

Dear Air Commodore Pickerd,
Further to our letter of 17 May in reply to
yours of 12 May
1972, I enclose copy of a report furnished
by Inspector RA. Schlein of Port Lincoln.
It appears that there was no actual sighting of
a U.I.O. at Tooligie Hills in December last, and although
Inspector was aware of strange markings
did not consider there
os surelent evidence at that time
to connect them with a U.F.O.
Moreover,
as there was
already growth from the dislodged wheat
heads,
it seemed that
some time must have elapsed since the disturbance.
The remainder of the Inspector's report speaks for
itself and we are sorry that you have been hindered or
embarrassed by the lack of an earlier report.
Although members generally are already aware of the
necessity to report such matters for your information, a
further instruction will be issued by a notice in the Police
Gazette.
Yours sincerely,
(Enc.)
(J.G. Mckinna)
Commissioner of Police.

S.A. POLICE DEPARTMENE
Copy of report furnished by Inspector R.A. Schicin,
Officer-in-Charge,
No. 18 Division Western
Late in December, 1971, I received a telephone call
From
either in.
Habner of looligle Hills reporting
that
they had found a strange
'marking'
in one of their
fields of wheat, which they could not explain.
There was
definitely NO reporting of any sighting of an Unidentified
Flying Object or any other phenomena in the sky.
Mr. Habner's property is some 60 to 70 miles from Port
Lincoln.
Owing to the expense, I did not think a visit was
warranted.
Several days later, Mr. Habner spoke to me in Port
Lincoln and suggested the possibility of the markings in his
field of wheat being made by a 'Flying Saucer'.
My own
personal views on such matters are that they snould always be
treated with respect but at the same time most cautiously.
However, because
of Mr. Habner's insistence on
of the markings,
I informed the local
the
'strangeness'
press and a photographer
travelled to Tooligie Hills on Ist January, 1972, and
photographed the markings.
Photos Port the ma times on 132c/2 and story were included
I recall Mr. Habner stating that a strange feature of
the incident was that none of the straw or stubble showed
any signs of charring or bummarks.
A further feature was
that the wheat 'knocked' from the heads on to the ground had
sprouted and commenced growing again, which indicated that the
area had been disturbed at least three to four weeks prior to
At this time Mr. Habner had notified the U.F.O. Research
and Investigation Group of whom Nr. P.M. Powell is Director.
ur. Powel1 contacted the Port Lincoln Times on 6th Januarize
1972, requesting copies of photos taken,
and I understand
has been in conmunication with the Port Lincoln Times and the
Habners ever since.
At no time have I been aware that any Flying Object'
was seen or heard.
I did not treat this matter in the true
light of an Unidentified Flying Object.
I was aware that a
Resent near explained had of take peror the
intended to visit the scene.
I am also conversant with the people in this area,
local mess were in possession of the
'story' and they
correspondents for
strange markings has been wide spread as Mr.
the Adelaide daily papers Habner has been
pestered with phone calls from all over Australia and also
from the United Kingdom.
I feel this incident did not come within the scope of
the usual sightings.
For these reasons I took no further action
in the matter.

GENERAL STATEMENT IN RELATION TO
UNIDENTIFIED FLYING
OBJECTS
35 A
1.
Your report on the sighting of an Unusual Flying Object (UPO)
has been investigated and the object was
has marten..
2.
You may be interested in the investigation of reports in Australia,
the United States and the United Kingdom and the following infornation provides
a brief outline of the subject.
3.
The investigation of reports of UFOs in Australia is carried out by
the Royal Australian Air Force, Directorate of Air Force Intelligence, at the
Department of Air in Canberra. A considerable amount of effort is spent in
investigating each report and the majority of observers are interviewed by
selected RAAF personnel.
4-
Between 23 January 60 and 26 May 71, the RAAF received 572 UFO
reports. Department of Air has assessed that 93 percent were explainable by
present scientific knowledge. Six percent of the reports did not provide
sufficient information to pernit proper analysis and
evaluation. One percent
of the reports were attributed to unknown causes.
5.
The United Kingdom Air Ministry has stated
that on an average, 90
percent of the sightings it investigates are explained by causes ranging from
meteorological balloons and meteors to aircraft lights. The other 10 percent
probably remain unexplained because of lack of reliable information.
6.
In 1966 the University of Colorado, under contract to the USA,
spent two years processing and investigating all American reports. Its
findings have been published by Bantam Books as "The Condon Report!. Its
general conclusion is that 'nothing has come from the study of UFOs in the
past 21 years that has added to scientifie knowledge and that further
extensive study of UPOs probably cannot be justified in the expectation that
science will be advanced thereby'. A panel of eminent scientists, chosen by
the US National Acadeny of Sciences, has examined and endorsed the Condon Reporta
/7. The

===== PAGE 91 =====
{"alpha_words": 123, "chars": 832, "method": "ocr", "page": 91, "variant": "plain-vision"}
-2-
7.
The USAF "Froject Blue Book! investigation of unidentified aerial
objects between 1953 and 1965 analysed 7,641 UFO reports. It concluded that:
80 percent of sightings were natural phenomena,
hoaxes, birds or man-made objects;
b.
17 percent of sightings provided insufficient
data
to permit thorough analysis and evaluation;
and
c.
three percent were unidentified.
8.
US and Soviet space exploration has found no evidence to support
the theory of life on planets in our solar system. It seems that
the Mariner
series of space exploration to Mars has proved it
a 'dead planet.
The only
other source of extra-terrestrial life, then, would have to be in another
"solar system'. It would seem, therefore, that whilst it may be possible for
extra-terrestrial life forms to visit Earth, it is improbable.
Department of Air
CANBERRA

CONDRAL STATEMENT IN RELATION TO
UNIDENTIFIED
FLYING OBJECTS
1.
Your report on the sighting of an Unusual Flying Object (UFO)
has been investigated and the object has. not.
ben.
. detamined at the love
...
2.
You may be interested in the investigation of reports in Australia,
the United States and the United Kingdom and the following information provides
a brief outline of the subject.
The investigation of reports of UFOs in Australia is carried out by
the Royal Australian Air Force, Directorate of Air Force Intelligence, at the
Department of Air in Canberra. A considerable amount of effort is spent in
investigating each report and the majority of observers are interviewed by
selected RAAF personnel.
4•
Between 23 January 60 and 26 May 71, the RAAF received 572 UFO
reports. Department
of Air has assessed that 93 percent were explainable by
present scientific knowledge. Six percent of the reports did not provide
sufficient information to pernit proper analysis and evaluation. One percent
of the reports were attributed to unknown causes.
5.
The United Kingdom Air Ministry has stated that on an average, 90
percent of the sightings it investigates are explained by causes ranging from
meteorological balloons and meteors to aircraft lighte. The other 10 percent
probably remain unexplained because of lack of reliable information.
6.
In 1966 the University of Colorado, under contract to the USAF,
spent two years processing and investigating all American reports. Its
findings have been published by Bantam Books as The Condon Report'. Its
general conclusion is that 'nothing has come from the study of UFOs in the
past 21 years that has added to scientific knowledge' and that further
extensive study of UFOs probably cannot be justified in the expectation that
science will be advanced thereby'. A panel of eminent scientists, chosen by
the US National Academy of Sciences, has examined and endorsed
the Condon Report.
/7. The

===== PAGE 106 =====
{"alpha_words": 123, "chars": 831, "method": "ocr", "page": 106, "variant": "plain-vision"}
-2-
7.
The USAR Project Blue Book! investigation of unidentified aerial
objects between 1953 and 1965 analysed 7,641 UFO reports, It concluded
that:
a.
80 percent of sightings were natural phenomena,
hoaxes, birds or man-made objects;
8.
b.
17 percent of sightings provided insufficient data
to permit thorough analysis and evaluation; and
three percent were unidentified.
US and Soviet space exploration has found no evidence to support
the theory of life on planets in our solar system. It seems that the Mariner
series of space exploration to Mars has proved it a dead' planet.
The only
other source of extra-terrestrial life, then, would have to be in another
'aolar system'. It would seem, therefore, that whilst it may be possible for
extra-terrestrial life forms to visit Earth, it is improbable.
Department of Air
CANBERRA

COUNTER-INTELLIGENCE -
UNIDENTIBIED FLYING OBJECTS
Enclosed is an UFO report forwarded to this Headquarters
by "The Australian Flying Saucer Research Society - (Adelaide)",
together with an accompanying letter from Mr John Burford which,
inter alia, outlines recent moves to amalgamate the various UHO
"research" societies in South Australia.
"investigating co-ordinator" for the AFSAS.
As on many occasions in the past, the report arrived
at this Headquarters too late to make an investigation possible
without considerable embarrassment and possible adverse publicity.
The various UFO societies in this State, while aware of the RAAF's
responsibility to investigate UFO sightings in an official capacity,
are nonetheless reluctant to pass on information on UFO sightings
to the RAAF until they have "picked the bones clean".
has been made by this Headquarters to elicit the co-operation of
local UFO organizations, and in particular the AFSHS, in an endeavour
to gain some first-band information on UFO sightings.
From conversations with Mr Burford and others, it would
appear that RAAF policy on non-recognition of unofficial UFO groups
restrictions on release of infornation (eg, military aircraft
movements) to them, are in no small way responsible for the lack
of "co-operation" experienced by
this Headquarters in the past.
Mr Burford himself, however, unlike his fellow AFSRS member, Mr
seems interested in co-operating with the RAAF "with
no strings attached" and to date has not attempted to make political
capital or to gain favourable publicity for his organization by his
dealings with this Headquarters.
Also enclosed for your information is a list of alleged
sightings investigated by the APSRS alone in 1971.
It is significant
that of the 112 sights, not one was reported to this Headquarters in
the first instance.
Indeed, it was only at the personal whim of
Mr Norris that the RAAF received copies of investigations (without
"findings") in the long term.
It would appear that, in spite of
sparse and rather patronising publicity by the mass media to the
effect that the RAAF is the responsible UTO-reporting organization,
and arrangements with the police to have any individual sighting a
UPO contact this Headquarters, the public at large in this State
remains either ignorant of the correct procedure, or chooses to
contact the more glamorous - and credulous - "flying saucer" society.
RESTRICTED
/Furthermore

lect: Personal comments on report of 7.5.720 Eirkenhead.
Author: Keith Basterfield 19.3.72
1. As the witness put on his glasses, to focus short range objects,
(e.g. 100m) and described the object with them on then this suggests
that the object was around 100m or so away, otherwise the object in
short range glasses would have been blurred had it been a lot further
away. The apparent reflection of ground light sources also supports
thus close range idea.
2. The angular velocity is approximately 180° in 20-30 seconds, this
means sither between 6-g°
per second.
For comparison, the Russian
booster rocket of 11.2.72 travelled 70° in 25 seconds, 1.o. about 3°/sec.
i-second is really travelling. This factor also suggests a close
range, for if the object were a long way away and doing 6-5/sec then
it would be covering a large distance in those 20-30 secs and hence at
a very high speed.
3. The angular diameter estimated as 2° by the interviewer also makes
a good cace for a close range object. Had the object been at a mile or
two away, then it would have been many tens of feet in diameter. At
about 100 metres tois would make it at least 10 foot in diameter.
4. The above four points, i.e. glasses, reflection, angular velocity
ano size appe = to incicate a close range for this object.

its bearing being 360°.As It paises 200-250° azimuth it accelerates
and is seen to be 2º angular diameter, and apparently reflecting
grounn light sources. It cóntinues to 00s°azimuth, 30° elevation
unere it banks swiftly cast then west and climbs, to be lost to
sight at 00s° azimuth, 6 50° elevation at an average angular velocity
of 6-5/second.
2. From the details given the object does not appear to have been:
(a) = meteor, (b) a balloon, (c) a satellite, or (d) an aircraft.

On the cetails given I an unable to offer a rational explanation,

GENERAL STATEMENT IN RELATION TO
UNIDENTIFIED
FLYING OBJECTS
1.
Your report on the sighting of an Unusual Flying Object (UPO)
has been investigated and the object was porchly a FORKER FRIENDSHA
2.
You may be interested in the investigation of reports in Australia,
the United States and
the United Kingdon and the following information provides
a brief outline of
the subject.
3.
The investigation of reports of UFOs in Australia is carried out by
the Royal Australian Air Force, Directorate of Air Force Intelligence, at the
Department of Air in Canberra. A considerable amount of effort is spent in
investigating each report and the majority of observers are interviewed by
selected RAAF personnel.
4.
Between 23 January 60 and 26 May 71, the RAAF received 572 UFO
reports. Department of Air has assessed that 93 percent were explainable by
present scientific knowledge. Six pereent of the reports did not provide
sufficient information to permit proper analysis and evaluation. One percent
of the reports were attributed to unknown causes.
The United Kingdom Air Ministry has stated that on an average, 90
percent of the sightings it investigates are explained by causes ranging from
meteorological balloons and meteors to aircraft lights. The other 10 percent
probably remain unexplained because of lack of reliable information.
6.
In 1966 the University of Colorado, under contract to the USAF,
spent two years processing and investigating all American reports. Its
findings have been published by Bantam Books as *The Condon Report. Its
general conclusion is that nothing has come from the study of UFOs in the
past 21 years that has added to scientific knowledge and that further
extensive study of UFOs probably cannot be justified in the expectation that
science will be advanced thereby'. A panel of eminent scientists, chosen by
the US National Academy of Sciences, has examined and endorsed the Condon Reporte
/1. The

The USAF Project Blue Book investigation of unidentified
aerial
objects between 1953 and 1965 analysed 7,641 UFO reports. It concluded that:

US and Soviet space exploration has found no evidence to support
the theory of life on planets in our solar system. It seems that the Mariner
series of space exploration to Mars has proved it a 'dead' planet. The only
other source of extra-terrestrial life, then, would have to be in another
"solar system'. It would seem, therefore, that whilst it may be possible for
extra-terrestrial life forms to visit Earth, it is improbable.

QUEENSIAND
As can be ascertained from the amplifying letter, which is
enclosed, the object was sighted slightly north of west, low on the
to the viewer's position.
The observer was driving
quite fast in a south-westerly direction and was positioned due west of
Mount White on the Road Mareeba-Dimbulan•
3.
facte:
Investigations into the incident have revealed the following
4.
b.
at the time of the sighting 2045k ens would have been
on a relative bearing of about 285
from the viewer's
position, with an altitude of 10°
These figures were
from the current Almanac, which further indicated
that at this time the planet would be at its brightest;
the local wind at the time was south-east at 40 knots;
and
Com
there was heavy broken cloud in the area.
The conclusions draw are:
a. the object seen Was Venus;
b.
the strong wind blowing broken clouds would have given
the object apparent novement;
and
C+
the clouds at times partially obscuring the object would
have made the light diffused, elightly changing in colour
and larger then it actually was, as well as completely
rendering impossible any accurate judging of distance.

exception that on the second night we saw a-pale red
light at the bottom of the larger bright light.
On the third night the light hovered more or less in the same
area for about twenty minutes, During this time I was able to
borrow a pair of binoculars which gave us a clearer view of
the object which seemed crystal shaped with a long narrow
shadow on the right hand side reaching nearly to the centre
of the light.
The shadow later moved more to the top of the
light but, still on the right hand side.
the larger 11ght
on the bobton lert hand corner, moving away
from and then returning towards the larger light, sometimes
dissapearing completely behind the larger light, moving much
faster than the larger light.
This smaller light was a bright pink in colour and the size
of the sammer light was like a ping-pong ball beside a
ball in comparison with the larger light.
After about twenty minutes, the larger yellow light moved
away in a similar pattern
as the two previous nights, leaving
no trace of the smaller pink light.
